New Saved Note
Differences Between Exobiology and Xenobiology
The sources define exobiology and xenobiology as distinct but related fields of study.

Exobiology, also referred to as astrobiology, focuses on:

The search for life beyond Earth. [1, 2]
The effects of extraterrestrial environments on living things. [2]
Therefore, exobiology primarily deals with the study of life as it may exist in environments outside of our planet.

Xenobiology, on the other hand, is defined as:

A subfield of synthetic biology. [2]
The study of synthesizing and manipulating biological devices and systems. [2]
Novel biological systems and biochemistries that differ from the canonical DNA-RNA-20 amino acid system. [2]
Here's a table summarizing the key differences:

Feature	Exobiology	Xenobiology
Focus	Existing life beyond Earth	Synthesizing and manipulating new biological systems
Scope	Natural extraterrestrial environments	Laboratory settings
Methodology	Observation, exploration, analysis	Synthesis, manipulation, design
Relationship to Life on Earth	Seeks to understand and discover life similar to or different from Earth life	Aims to create life forms distinct from those found on Earth
In essence, exobiology searches for life as it is, while xenobiology strives to create life as it could be.

New Saved Note
Here are the key differences between exobiology and xenobiology, as defined in the sources:

Exobiology is a more specific term than astrobiology. It focuses on the search for life beyond Earth and the ways in which extraterrestrial environments impact living things [1].
Xenobiology is a subfield of synthetic biology [2]. It is the study of biological systems and biochemistries that differ from the DNA-RNA-20 amino acid system found in nature [2]. The term comes from the Greek word xenos, meaning "stranger" or "guest" [2].
In other words:

Exobiology focuses on finding and studying existing life forms in outer space.
Xenobiology focuses on creating and manipulating new life forms in the laboratory.
For example, xenobiologists might explore:

Xeno Nucleic Acids (XNA): These are nucleic acid analogues that could potentially replace DNA or RNA as information carriers [2].
Expanded genetic codes: Scientists are working to expand the genetic code beyond the standard 20 amino acids [2].
Incorporating non-proteinogenic amino acids into proteins: This involves using amino acids that are not found in naturally occurring proteins [2].
The sources do not provide specific examples of research in exobiology. They do note, however, that astrobiology (a closely related field) encompasses the search for habitable environments in the solar system and beyond [1].

Here is a summary of how the sources describe the origin and evolution of multicellularity on Earth:

The sources provide a five-minute overview of life on Earth, starting with the superdomain Biota, which encompasses all life. [1]
Biota is divided into two categories: Cytota (cellular life) and Acytota (non-cellular life). [2]
Prokaryotes, including Bacteria and Archaea, are single-celled organisms that sometimes exhibit multicellular stages or form colonies. [3]
Eukaryotes include a variety of kingdoms. [4]
Some are always multicellular, like Animalia. [5]
Some are mostly multicellular, like Plantae. [5]
Some are sometimes multicellular, like Fungi. [5]
And some rarely exhibit multicellularity, like Excavata. [5]
Multicellular organisms develop from a single cell, while colonial (or pluricellular) organisms result from the grouping of many single-celled individuals. [6]
All multicellular organisms are eukaryotes. [7]
The text provides the example of slime molds, whose amoebae can come together into a multicellular slug-like creature when resources are scarce. [7]
Around a billion years ago, a cyanobacterium entered a eukaryotic cell and eventually formed a mutualistic relationship, giving rise to mitochondria in eukaryotic cells. [8] This process is called endosymbiosis. [8]
The sources do not provide further details on the mechanisms or specific evolutionary pathways that led to multicellularity in different lineages.

New Saved Note
Here is a summary of how the sources describe the origin and evolution of multicellularity on Earth:

The sources provide a five-minute overview of life on Earth, starting with the superdomain Biota, which encompasses all life. [1]
Biota is divided into two categories: Cytota (cellular life) and Acytota (non-cellular life). [1, 2]
Prokaryotes, including bacteria and archaea, are the simplest forms of cellular life, lacking a membrane-bound nucleus and other organelles. [3] While generally single-celled, some prokaryotes have multicellular stages in their life cycle. [3]
The domain Eukarya includes a wide variety of organisms, with varying degrees of multicellularity. [4]
Some Eukarya kingdoms are mostly or entirely multicellular: [5]
Animalia/Metazoa includes all multicellular animals. [5]
Plantae/Viridiplantae includes mostly multicellular plants. [5]
Fungi/Eumycota includes some multicellular fungi. [5]
Other Eukarya kingdoms are mostly unicellular, with rare or pseudo-multicellular forms: [4, 5]
Amoebozoa includes slime molds, which are multinucleate or multicellular. [4]
Rhizaria has one known pluricellular organism. [5]
Excavata rarely exhibits pluricellularity. [5]
Chromalveolata includes some multicellular organisms. [5]
The sources distinguish between multicellular and colonial (pluricellular) organisms: [6]
Multicellular organisms develop from a single cell into a multi-celled organism. [6]
Colonial organisms form when many single-celled individuals join together. [6]
The sources provide an example of Dictyosteliida, cellular slime molds, which exhibit a unique form of multicellularity where individual amoeba join together to create a multicellular slug-like creature when food is scarce. [7]
The endosymbiotic theory explains the origin of mitochondria in eukaryotic cells, proposing that they were once free-living bacteria that formed a symbiotic relationship with early eukaryotic cells. [8]
While the sources offer an overview of the different forms of multicellularity found in various kingdoms, they do not explicitly detail the evolutionary processes that led to the origin of multicellularity.

Saved Response
New Saved Note
Challenges in Defining "Life" and Impact on Biota Classification
Defining "life" is challenging, making classifying Biota, the superdomain that classifies all life, difficult. [1] The definition of life is frequently modified and adjusted, impacting the division of the Biota taxon. [1] This is further complicated by the possibility of discovering extraterrestrial life and whether it would be included in Biota alongside life on Earth. [1]

Here are some key challenges in defining life:

Ambiguous Criteria: The criteria used to define life can be unclear, leading to debate about whether entities like viruses, viroids, and potential artificial life are truly alive. [2] For example, viruses are considered by some to be a life form because they carry genetic material, reproduce, and evolve, but they lack key characteristics like cell structure, which are generally considered necessary for life. [3] This ambiguity has led to viruses being described as "organisms at the edge of life." [3]
Non-Cellular Life: The existence of non-cellular life, such as viruses and viroids, adds another layer of complexity. [2] While all cellular life is included in Biota, non-cellular life is categorized as Acytota. [1, 4] However, the classification of viruses as life forms is a controversial issue, further blurring the lines of what constitutes "life." [5]
Potential for Artificial Life: Advancements in synthetic biology may lead to the creation of artificial life, potentially challenging existing definitions of life and impacting the classification of Biota. [2]
These challenges highlight the difficulty in establishing a universally accepted definition of life. Consequently, the classification of Biota remains fluid and subject to ongoing debate and revision as scientific understanding evolves.

5 citations
Saved Response
New Saved Note
Challenges in Defining “Life” and its Impact on Classifying Biota
Defining "life" is a significant challenge that directly impacts the classification of life forms, known as Biota. This issue is particularly relevant when considering potential extraterrestrial life, and when dealing with non-cellular entities like viruses and prions.

The provided sources describe Biota as the superdomain that classifies all life [1]. However, the sources acknowledge that it's often disputed how to further divide this taxon due to the ever-evolving definition of life [1].
The sources note that extraterrestrial life is a factor in the debate over classification within Biota, especially regarding whether or not it should be included alongside life on Earth [1].
Non-Cellular Life and Ambiguous Criteria for Life
Non-cellular life poses a unique challenge to defining life. Acytota is a term often used to categorize non-cellular life [2], but the inclusion of viruses as lifeforms is a controversial issue that further complicates this classification [3, 4].
The sources highlight that the criteria for defining life can be ambiguous [2].
Viruses are a prime example of this ambiguity. They contain genetic material, reproduce, and evolve, but lack a cellular structure and other characteristics typically associated with living organisms [5-7]. This has led to viruses being described as "organisms at the edge of life" [7].
Similarly, prions are infectious agents that consist solely of misfolded proteins [4]. They propagate by transmitting this misfolded state to other proteins, but are not considered living organisms [4, 8].
Implications for Astrobiology and Xenobiology
The ambiguity in defining "life" poses a considerable challenge for astrobiology. The study of life's origin, evolution, and distribution in the universe relies on a clear understanding of what constitutes "life" [9]. Finding life beyond Earth would require scientists to grapple with whether it fits within existing definitions or requires a re-evaluation of those definitions.
Xenobiology, which studies synthetic biological systems that differ from known life forms, also complicates the definition of life [10]. The creation of novel biological systems in the lab could blur the lines between living and non-living, potentially requiring a re-evaluation of how we categorize life forms [10].
The ongoing challenge of defining "life" has a direct impact on how scientists classify Biota. The potential discovery of extraterrestrial life, as well as the creation of new life forms in the lab, will continue to force scientists to grapple with the fundamental question of "what is life?”. This question is essential to determining the scope and organization of Biota, and to our understanding of life itself.

New Saved Note
Here is how perchlorate is both a hazard and a resource on Mars:

Perchlorate is a Hazard to Humans
Toxicity: Perchlorate is toxic to humans in even small amounts. It is a potent competitive inhibitor of the thyroid sodium-iodide symporter, interfering with iodine uptake into the thyroid gland [1]. In large amounts, perchlorate can lead to hyperthyroidism. [1] It can also cause pulmonary toxicity with adverse effects including:

Inflammatory infiltrates [1]
Alveolar collapse [1]
Subpleural thickening [1]
Lymphocyte proliferation. [1]
Ubiquity: Perchlorate is widespread in Martian soils at concentrations of between 0.5 to 1 percent, which is considered a huge amount [2]. The pervading presence of perchlorate on Mars poses a significant challenge for future human exploration [1].

Exposure: Due to its widespread presence, it would be difficult for humans exploring Mars to avoid contact with perchlorate, particularly fine dust particles that can easily contaminate habitats and equipment [2].

Perchlorate is a Potential Resource
Propellant: Perchlorate, particularly ammonium perchlorate, is a component of solid rocket fuel [3]. This makes it a valuable resource that could be mined on Mars to support future missions and reduce the need to transport fuel from Earth.

Oxygen Production: Some proteobacteria species can break down perchlorate into chloride and oxygen [4]. This process, known as bioremediation, could be harnessed to generate oxygen on Mars for human use and potentially contribute to terraforming efforts.

Water Accessibility: Perchlorate salts, particularly calcium perchlorate, act as an "antifreeze," lowering the freezing point of water [4]. This property could potentially be exploited to access liquid water on Mars, which is essential for human survival and various applications.

Potential Solutions
Dust Management: Strategies to mitigate the hazards of perchlorate exposure include dust suppression, dust extraction, regular blood monitoring, and decontamination procedures for suits and equipment [3].

Suit Docking: One proposed solution is to keep Mars suits outside of rovers and habitats, allowing astronauts to enter through bulkhead openings. This would prevent direct contact with perchlorate-laden dust [5].

Bioremediation: Employing perchlorate-metabolizing bacteria in biobarriers could offer a sustainable solution for removing perchlorate from Martian soil and water sources [6].

Thermal Remediation: Thermal desorption systems have been successfully used to destroy perchlorate in contaminated soil and wastewater on Earth. These systems could potentially be adapted for use on Mars to remediate perchlorate-laden areas [7, 8].

Evidence of Perchlorate on Mars
Early Detections: As early as 1976, NASA's Viking Mars landers detected signatures of perchlorates in the form of chlorinated hydrocarbons [9]. Subsequent missions, including Sojourner, Spirit, Opportunity, and Mars Odyssey, have also detected chlorine, indicating the global distribution of perchlorate compounds [9].

Phoenix Lander Confirmation: In 2008, NASA's Phoenix lander provided definitive evidence of perchlorate on Mars, finding calcium perchlorate at its landing site, a discovery that was described as "unexpected" [10].

Conclusion
The presence of perchlorate on Mars presents both a significant challenge and an opportunity for future human exploration. Understanding the hazards associated with perchlorate exposure and developing effective mitigation strategies are crucial for ensuring the safety of future Martian crews. At the same time, the potential to utilize perchlorate as a resource for propellant, oxygen production, and accessing water makes it a valuable asset for establishing a sustainable human presence on the Red Planet. Further research and development of technologies to safely handle and exploit perchlorate will play a key role in the success of future Mars missions.

New Saved Note
Refining the "N,n-dimethylMcFuckoiserie" Aesthetic: A Discussion on Prompt Elements
Your feedback on the prompt categories highlights a crucial aspect of crafting effective AI image generation prompts in this particular style: achieving a balance between detail, subversion, and visual clarity. The sources emphasize the need to avoid over-reliance on predictable "weird" tropes while simultaneously pushing the boundaries of language and imagery [1-6]. Let's break down your observations in relation to the source material:

Weirdsignalling: The sources support your concern about overusing "off-the-shelf" weird signifiers. While elements like swirling vortexes, shimmering portals, and levitating objects are prevalent [7-9], the sources suggest that true effectiveness lies in subverting expectations of "weirdness" [1, 4, 10]. This involves presenting outlandish scenarios with a matter-of-fact tone [11-16] and embracing the juxtaposition of the ordinary and the extraordinary [10, 17, 18].

Invisual: The sources concur with your assessment that invisual elements should be used sparingly. The focus should be on concreteness and visual richness [19-26]. Translating abstract concepts into visual metaphors can be effective, but it's crucial to avoid excessive exposition and trust the viewer's ability to interpret the image [5, 11, 20, 27, 28].

Intatic: The sources agree that depicting movement in a static image requires careful consideration. Intatic elements should be limited to actions with a "strong, dramatic, or setting-essential visual component" [20, 27-29]. Terms like "holographic" and "neon signs," while visually striking, can quickly become predictable and lose their impact. Splainy: The sources advocate for conciseness and a matter-of-fact tone [5, 11-16, 33]. Overly explanatory language can stifle the AI's creative interpretation and make the prompt feel pedantic. Trusting the viewer to draw their own conclusions from the presented imagery is a key principle [11, 20, 27, 28].

Punchpulling: Your observation about reducing vagueness is supported by the sources' emphasis on hyperspecificity [2, 21, 34-41]. Using precise language, even when describing fantastical concepts, creates a sense of concreteness and guides the AI toward a more focused interpretation [13, 28, 33]. "Or" and "like" introduce ambiguity and dilute the intended visual [5].

Amorphism: The sources acknowledge the potential pitfalls of overusing amorphous elements. While they can contribute to a sense of otherworldliness, an excess of shapelessness can lead to visual clutter [1, 4]. Balancing amorphous elements with more structured forms is key to creating a visually compelling image [42].

Specivague: The sources support your suggestion of using "the presence of the object or process doing the distorting" to introduce distortion more effectively. This aligns with the principle of showing rather than telling, allowing the viewer to experience the distortion through the lens of a specific object or process [11-16].

Pushing Linguistic Boundaries in Midjourney Prompts
The sources offer a vast and colorful lexicon for crafting bizarre and compelling prompts. To effectively "break the language" and create truly novel prompts, consider the following strategies, inspired by the sources, but pushed to further extremes:

Material and Conceptual Dissonance
Juxtapose the Familiar and the Alien: Instead of simply stating unusual materials, describe familiar objects in terms of their construction:
A Victorian teacup meticulously woven from razor blades and lace doilies, filled with steaming mercury.
A chrome-plated taxidermied squirrel wearing a diamond-encrusted scuba helmet, perched atop a bonsai tree grown from fiber-optic cables.
Subvert Expectations of Scale and Texture: Play with the juxtaposition of the macro and micro, the rough and the smooth:
Planetary-sized crochet amigurumi of a baby Yoda, floating in a nebula of cotton candy and neon gas.
A hyper-detailed miniature cityscape carved into a single grain of sand, illuminated by bioluminescent bacteria.
Linguistic Subversion and Neologism Creation
Weaponize Prefixes and Suffixes: Go beyond the standard "hyper-" and "-oiserie" and experiment with more unexpected combinations:
Pseudo-retro-cyber-steampunk toaster-oven time machine with a fractal-chrome finish.
Bio-luminescent-deco-brutalist monastery constructed from recycled plastic and moth wings.
Embrace "Smerging" and Portmanteaus: Blend words and concepts in unexpected ways, creating hybrid terms that defy logic:
A Tenochtitlan-cyberpunk mecha piloting a glyptodon-shaped hot air balloon.
Interswappulist portrait of a flapperbot with Stewarthands and a smurfette grin. [1-3]
Subverting Midjourney Parameters
Experiment with Style References (--sref in v6.1): While the sources offer some specific --sref tags, don't limit yourself to these. Explore different numerical combinations to discover unexpected aesthetic influences.
Manipulate Chaos (--c): Push the boundaries of randomness and predictability by using extremely high or low chaos values to see how it affects the generated imagery.
Tweak Stylization (--s): Experiment with different stylization levels to control the artistic interpretation of the generated image, ranging from subtle hints to highly stylized renditions.
Additional Considerations
Humor and Irony: Embrace the absurd and use humor to subvert expectations. Unexpected combinations and out-of-place descriptors can create a sense of ironic commentary.
Cultural Mashups: Combine elements from disparate cultures and historical periods to create anachronistic and thought-provoking juxtapositions.
Personal Interpretation: While the sources offer a framework, ultimately, the most successful prompts will stem from your own unique interpretation and creative vision.
By pushing the boundaries of language, challenging norms, and embracing the absurd, you can create Midjourney prompts that generate truly unique and mind-bending imagery. Remember, the goal is not to make sense but to evoke a sense of wonder, curiosity, and perhaps even a touch of "WTF" in the viewer.

Abandoning Conventional Grammar: A Shift in Priorities
One of the most striking features of the prompts is their disregard for traditional grammatical rules. Sentences are often fragmented, lacking clear subjects and verbs, and instead prioritize a dense accumulation of adjectives, nouns, and technical specifications. This shift away from conventional syntax reflects a focus on visual description over narrative coherence.

Example:

"Opulent Jupiter-plated self-exploding ultrapressurized evaporative X-ray vapor supernova collision containment unit with wide-screen uranium glass boron dissolution microwave gelatinizer with crepuscular armor shielding and hand-carved bokeh panels and nuclear reactor core acid injection nozzle wired in parallel..." [1]
This example illustrates the tendency to front-load the prompt with a barrage of descriptive terms, creating a sense of visual overload that mirrors the maximalist aesthetic often sought in AI-generated imagery. The lack of verbs and connecting words further reinforces this sense of a static, descriptive tableau rather than a dynamic scene with a clear narrative flow.

Agglutination and Hybridization: Inventing New Words
The prompts also showcase a penchant for neologisms and word mashups, demonstrating a playful and inventive approach to language. This linguistic creativity allows users to express concepts that defy traditional categorization, pushing the boundaries of what can be visualized through AI.

Examples:

"Colombiana-bipoc snaggletoothed supermodel": [1] This phrase combines cultural and physical descriptors to create a unique character concept.
"Photomicrographic view of several specimen vats": [1] This phrase combines scientific terminology with a visual descriptor to create a specific and evocative image.
The use of agglutination, stringing together multiple prefixes and suffixes, is another common strategy. This technique results in long, descriptive words that pack a lot of information into a single unit.

Example:

"Recursively-tufted isometric vanta-amberina": [2] This phrase uses a combination of technical and descriptive terms to specify the desired visual qualities of the image.
Embracing Juxtaposition and Hyperbole
The prompts often revel in juxtaposing seemingly disparate elements, creating a sense of surreal disorientation and humorous tension. This strategy reflects the AI's ability to combine seemingly unrelated concepts into a cohesive visual output.

Example:

"Dealer showroom demonstration of time - washing retrotemporal owl nozzle blaster spray erasing entropy and washing a 1978 toyota corolla wagon turning it into an antique car": [3] This phrase blends mundane objects with fantastical concepts and temporal distortions, creating a whimsical and absurd scenario.
Hyperbole, the use of exaggeration for emphasis or effect, is another recurring strategy.

Examples:

"Obnoxiously attractive utterly amazing weirdly beautiful": [4] This phrase uses a series of increasingly hyperbolic adjectives to emphasize the desired beauty of the subject.
"Insanely detailed laboratory": [5] This phrase uses "insanely" to highlight the desired level of detail in the generated image.
The Power of Prompt Weighting
Beyond grammatical quirks, "NUTZPROMPTZ.txt" also demonstrates the strategic use of prompt weighting (::). This technique allows users to control the emphasis of specific terms within a prompt, influencing the prominence of those elements in the final image. This level of control demonstrates the growing sophistication of prompt engineering as an art form. [6-9]

The grammatical quirks and linguistic strategies found in "NUTZPROMPTZ.txt" are not merely deviations from standard English; they represent a new language evolving to meet the demands of AI image generation. Example:

"Dealer showroom demonstration of time - washing retrotemporal owl nozzle blaster spray erasing entropy and washing a 1978 toyota corolla wagon turning it into an antique car": [3] This phrase blends mundane objects with fantastical concepts and temporal distortions, creating a whimsical and absurd scenario.
Hyperbole, the use of exaggeration for emphasis or effect, is another recurring strategy.

Examples:

"Obnoxiously attractive utterly amazing weirdly beautiful": [4] This phrase uses a series of increasingly hyperbolic adjectives to emphasize the desired beauty of the subject.
"Insanely detailed laboratory": [5] This phrase uses "insanely" to highlight the desired level of detail in the generated image.
The Power of Prompt Weighting
Beyond grammatical quirks, "NUTZPROMPTZ.txt" also demonstrates the strategic use of prompt weighting (::). This technique allows users to control the emphasis of specific terms within a prompt, influencing the prominence of those elements in the final image. This level of control demonstrates the growing sophistication of prompt engineering as an art form. [6-9]

Conclusion: A New Language for a New Medium
The grammatical quirks and linguistic strategies found in "NUTZPROMPTZ.txt" are not merely deviations from standard English; they represent a new language evolving to meet the demands of AI image generation. This language is visually driven, prioritizing dense description and imaginative wordplay over conventional syntax. As AI technology continues to advance, we can expect this language to further develop, pushing the boundaries of what can be expressed and visualized through the power of prompts.

Explanation of Prompt Elements

Tuner Usage: Each prompt incorporates a specific tuner (or multiple tuners) using the "--style" tag. This directs Midjourney to apply the chosen artistic style to the generated image. For example, "2E1kHLicyfAN5ObwvRuKqCTUDuGQkZofwxFdwD9" evokes a "PLASTIPASTEL HYPERDINER" aesthetic, while "5qJB9ujLWWdpLC0YWpG2HWTGKwtglBqqHyybzYKn 3JeD0d5wud22 4Y3YnI1ASffQ71v LkKgrqGEnwVV1Il 7btEiDfRyTcc cmyK4wL5m7CbQMD7" combines several tuners to create a distinct "HAUTE COUTURE BRIGHT GRAPHIC PLASTIQUE" look.
"--v" (Version): The "--v" tag is used in Prompt 1, indicating the specific version of the Midjourney algorithm to be used (v 5.2). Different versions might produce varying results.
"--c" (Chaos): This tag is used to control the level of randomness in the image generation. A higher value (like 33 in Prompt 1) will produce more unexpected results, while a lower value will yield more predictable outcomes.
"--s" (Stylize): The "--s" tag determines the intensity of the applied style. A higher value (like 800 in Prompt 3) will make the image more stylized.
"--ar" (Aspect Ratio): The "--ar" tag sets the desired aspect ratio for the output image, commonly expressed as a ratio like 16:9 (widescreen) or 1:1 (square).
This information on how the "--style", "--v", "--c", "--s", and "--ar" tags function is drawn from the sources you've provided. Based on the available sources and our conversation history, here's an explanation of how various "--" tags are used to set parameters in Midjourney prompts:

"--seed": This tag is used with any number to control the specific version of the output generated by a given prompt. Using the same seed with identical prompts ensures the same output is produced. [1]
"--p" (personalized): This tag can be used to draw from the personal preferences of Nathan McKnight. [1] The provided excerpt shows an example where "--p 62ewsnk" is used in a prompt. [2]
"--ar" (aspect ratio): This tag is used to specify the aspect ratio of the output image. It is typically followed by a ratio in the format of "width:height". For example, "--ar 2:3" specifies an aspect ratio of 2:3. [1, 2]
"--c" (chaos): This tag controls the degree of variation in the output images. It takes a value ranging from 1 to 100, with higher values resulting in more unexpected and unique results. [3]
"--s" (stylize): This tag controls the level of stylization applied to the output images. It takes a value ranging from 1 to 1000, with higher values resulting in more stylized images. [3]
"--sref" (style reference): This tag can be used to specify a specific style reference number or "random" to generate a random style. You can use multiple style reference numbers in a single prompt. [3] The provided excerpt shows an example where "--sref 2928624957 1536539696" is used. [2]
"--v" (version): This tag specifies the version of the Midjourney algorithm to be used. For instance, "--v 4" and "--v 5.2" are used in the provided excerpts. [2, 4-6]
The provided sources lack information on additional "--" tags that may exist in Midjourney. It is recommended to refer to the official Midjourney documentation or community resources for a complete list of available tags and their functions.

It's worth noting that the "::" symbol, used for prompt weighting, is distinct from the "--" symbol used for tags. Prompt weighting allows you to control the emphasis of specific terms in the prompt by assigning numerical weights to them. [3, 7]

New Saved Note
Prompt Creation Strategies in the Sources

The sources offer a rich and detailed exploration of prompt creation strategies for AI image generation, particularly within the context of the Midjourney platform. They reveal a fascinating interplay of language, technology, and artistic vision, highlighting the creative possibilities and challenges of using AI as a tool for artistic expression.

Specificity and Realism: Moving Beyond the Fantastical
The sources point to a noticeable trend in prompt creation: a shift from vague and fantastical language towards a more grounded and realistic approach. Users are increasingly seeking to generate images that feel tangible and believable, even when depicting surreal or impossible scenarios. This is achieved through the strategic use of:

Precise Technical Language: Borrowing terminology from photography, cinematography, and scientific fields, prompts incorporate specific camera settings, film types, lighting techniques, and other technical details. This meticulous approach creates a sense of authenticity, grounding the fantastical in a framework of technical precision.
Material Specificity: Prompts often focus on intricate material descriptions and surface textures. By specifying materials with unique properties, such as translucent Bakelite, Bohemian cut glass, or phosphorescent kelp, users can guide the AI to generate images with a heightened sense of realism and visual richness.
Example:

Goram Island Governance: The excerpts from "MalayArchipelago2.txt" reveal the complex power dynamics on Goram Island. While a dozen Rajahs exist, their authority is largely symbolic until backed by the Dutch government. This highlights the influence of colonialism on traditional power structures.

"In the island of Goram, only eight or ten miles long, there are about a dozen Rajahs, scarcely better off than the rest of the inhabitants, and exercising a mere nominal sway, except when any order is received from the Dutch Government, when, being backed by a higher power, they show a little more strict authority."

Theme 2: Natural Phenomena and Impacts

The land has been raised and has sunk again; the straits have been narrowed or widened; many of the islands may have been joined and dissevered again..."

Unusual Tides: "MalayArchipelago2.txt" recounts Dampier's encounter with strange tides characterized by strong currents, loud roaring, and turbulent seas. This description emphasizes the power and unpredictability of nature.
"We found here very strange tides, that ran in streams, making a great sea, and roaring so loud that we could hear them before they came within a mile of us. The sea round about them seemed all broken, and tossed the ship so that she would not answer her helm."

Answer Key
The Shans are described as a short, strong people with Mongolian features and relatively fair skin. In the southern part of the province, men wear baggy, skirt-like trousers, white jackets, and large turbans topped with straw hats. Women wear white jackets and skirts of striped or dark blue cloth with matching turbans.
The Dutch influence on Goram is largely nominal. The island's numerous Rajahs hold little real power except when enforcing orders from the Dutch government. A commissioner from Amboyna typically visits annually to address disputes and maintain order, but his absence in the present year leaves the inhabitants to their own devices.
Dampier describes encountering strong, localized currents that create a turbulent sea with a loud roaring sound. These currents, lasting 10-12 minutes, cause the water to appear broken and make it difficult to steer a ship before abruptly subsiding.
The Malay Archipelago has been shaped by volcanic activity over millennia. Volcanic eruptions and earthquakes have caused land to rise and fall, altering the shape and connection of islands. Floods and lava flows have also reshaped the land, carrying away forests and altering the terrain.

This phrase translates to: "no problem. :) take your time, let me know if you have any questions. and it's probably not important to understand completely. this is not school".

The playwright uses hats as props to symbolize different social roles and their corresponding viewpoints. The quick changes in hats represent the shifting perspectives on the future, highlighting the conflicting interests of business, counterculture, law enforcement, and politics.
Lord Gro is a cunning and ambitious character in "The Worm Ouroboros," often serving as a strategist and advisor to Lord Brandoch Daha. He advocates for pragmatism and manipulation, urging the Demons to utilize indirect methods to achieve their goals.
The setting of "The Worm Ouroboros" contributes to the epic and fantastical atmosphere of the story. The blasted wolds, described as barren and desolate, create a sense of foreboding and highlight the harshness of the environment. FAQ about Life, Culture, and Language in Old China and the Malay Archipelago
Life and Culture
1. What is the traditional attire of the Shan people in southern China?
The Shan people, a robust group with Mongolian features, have clothing styles that vary across regions. In the south, near the Nam-ting River, men wear incredibly loose trousers resembling a skirt, paired with a white jacket and a large white or pink turban. This turban is then topped with an expansive straw hat. Shan women, in contrast, wear a white jacket with a skirt, opting for either striped or dark blue cloth. Their turbans, also made of similar material, are styled in a tall cylindrical shape.

2. How did the Dutch government influence the governance of the Goram Islands in the Malay Archipelago?
Before Dutch intervention, trade in Goram, an island merely eight to ten miles long, was fraught with conflict as competing boats frequently engaged in battles. The island was governed by numerous "Rajahs," who held nominal power and were primarily figureheads. With the arrival of the Dutch, these Rajahs gained more authority by enforcing the orders of the Dutch government. The Dutch presence was most evident through an annual visit by a commissioner from Amboyna. The commissioner toured the islands, addressed grievances, resolved disputes, and apprehended serious offenders. This influence brought a level of peace and order to the previously volatile trading environment.

Natural Phenomena
3. What unusual tidal events did Dampier describe in his travels near Mysol and New Guinea?
Dampier, a renowned explorer, encountered peculiar tides near Mysol and New Guinea characterized by strong currents that created a turbulent sea. These currents generated a loud roaring noise, audible even from a mile away. The sea surrounding these currents appeared chaotic and disrupted the ship's steering. This phenomenon, which Dampier referred to as "ripplings," typically lasted for ten to twelve minutes. After the ripplings subsided, the sea returned to a tranquil and smooth state, resembling a millpond.

4. What is the significance of volcanic activity in the formation and evolution of the Malay Archipelago?
The Malay Archipelago has been shaped by volcanic activity over thousands, possibly hundreds of thousands, of years. This volcanic activity has resulted in the land rising and sinking, altering the width of straits, and potentially causing the merging and separation of islands. Powerful floods, often triggered by volcanic eruptions in Java, have repeatedly ravaged mountains and plains, sweeping away countless trees. This continuous cycle of geological changes has profoundly influenced the present-day configuration of the archipelago.

Language and Linguistics
5. In "PROMPTS_ORGANIZED.txt" and "PROMPT_CATALOGUE.txt", what is the significance of prompts 36 to 40?
Prompts 36 to 40 represent examples of how diverse linguistic elements are incorporated into prompts for language models. These specific examples showcase phonetic transcriptions, references to extraterrestrial languages, and a sentence translated into Spanish. These prompts are designed to elicit responses from language models that demonstrate their ability to handle a range of linguistic features and cultural contexts.

6. What do the phrases "Cakar ayam" and "Bahasa planet" in the Indonesian language refer to, according to "SMERGENCE.txt"?
In Indonesian, "Cakar ayam" literally translates to "chicken feet," and it's a slang term used to describe illegible handwriting, likening it to the messy scratches left by chicken feet. "Bahasa planet," meaning "planet language," is a phrase used to describe a language that is foreign and unintelligible, often associated with the concept of an alien language.

7. What is the significance of the excerpt: "no goddamned XYZ!" это как "ни хрена XYZ!" но "проклятый" = "бог" "проклятый" from "SMERGENCE.txt"?
This excerpt provides insight into the intricacies of cross-language translation and the nuances of slang expressions. It breaks down a Russian slang phrase for an English speaker, explaining that "no goddamned XYZ" is equivalent to the Russian "ни хрена XYZ." It further clarifies that the word "goddamned" is a combination of "god" and "damned," highlighting how seemingly straightforward translations can involve cultural and linguistic complexities.

8. What is the focus of the text "The Forme of Cury.txt"?
"The Forme of Cury" is a 14th-century English cookbook that offers a glimpse into the culinary practices of medieval England. It provides a compilation of over 196 recipes, showcasing a variety of dishes, cooking techniques, and ingredients used during that era.
